People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I haven’t really stopped afterward and thought about how I feel about it.\n\nDoctor: On those nights when you're eating in the kitchen, are you fully awake and aware of what you're doing, and do you have trouble falling asleep before that happens?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, to be honest. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m fully awake or how my sleep is beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that you have little appetite in the morning, but then end up eating most of your daily food in the evening and at night?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of inconsistent. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but other nights I end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep, eating crackers and sweets. My eating’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you been feeling persistently sad, down, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I go through stretches where everything in the house feels gray and heavy. I’ll just sit at the kitchen table feeling empty, even when nothing bad happened that day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these stretches of feeling sad, empty, or gray—days, weeks, or longer?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s added up to about seven months now—the gray, guilty times where I just lose interest in things.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, did this pattern of skipping dinner and then eating at night start around the same time as those gray, empty stretches, or was it happening before that?\n\nPatient: I think it was around then. My eating started getting really unpredictable—barely touching dinner some weeks, then other nights eating crackers and sweets in the kitchen after everyone was asleep. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: During these gray, empty stretches, have you also noticed feeling unusually tired or low on energy, or having trouble concentrating on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know I feel empty during those times, but I haven’t paid close attention to my energy or concentration.\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, have you experienced similar stretches of feeling down or empty in the past, on and off for years, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: It’s happened before, on and off. I’d have stretches where the whole house just feels gray and heavy, and I sit at the kitchen table feeling empty even though nothing bad even happened that day.\n\nDoctor: During those earlier stretches of feeling down or empty in the past, did you notice this same pattern of skipping dinner and then eating at night, or is that eating pattern new to this current episode?\n\nPatient: I think this eating pattern is more what pushed me to come in this time. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep, eating crackers and sweets.\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of skipping dinner and then eating at night caused you significant distress, or has it interfered with your daily life—like your work, relationships, or how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been bothering me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—the eating being all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers and sweets.\n\nDoctor: During times when you haven't been feeling down or empty, have you still noticed this same pattern of skipping dinner and then eating at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s just when I’m feeling down. My eating’s been all over the place—that’s what finally made me come in. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep, eating crackers and sweets.\n\nDoctor: About how many nights per week would you say you find yourself eating in the kitchen after everyone's asleep?\n\nPatient: It varies, but on those weeks, probably a few nights a week. I’ll skip dinner and then end up in the kitchen later, eating crackers or sweets.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
